CORAM: HONOURABLE MR. JUSTICE MIHIR KUMAR JHA
ORAL JUDGMENT
3 06-10-2015 Heard learned counsel for the parties.
2. In this batch of writ applications the petitioners,
Panchayat Rozgar Sevaks (P.R.S), have prayed for

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 84
quashing of orders terminating their contractual service as
well as advertisement issued for filling up the post of
P.R.S and consequential reinstatement in service.
3. Learned counsel for the petitioners have
basically concentrated on the aspect that though it may be
true that the post of PRS is a contractual assignment but
then as the same carries the concept of consolidated
monthly salary alike many other post in the Rural
Development Department of the State Govt. being
managed through the District Rural Development Agency
(DRDA), the entire working strength of the post of PRS
all over the State had launched initially a peaceful
agitation for improving their service condition and had
come out with certain legitimate grievance in course of
which they had also gone on strike. It has, however, been
sought to be impressed upon this Court that such agitation
including going on strike was peaceful in nature and when
the Government itself had conceded to some of their
legitimate demands by increasing monthly fixed

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 85
emoluments it ought to have not resorted to termination of
service of all the PRS all over the State simply because
they did not resume the duty by ending their strike.
According to them, such indiscriminate action on the part
of the respondents by not only removing all the PRS from
the service but also seeking to fill up the post of those
PRS by issuance of fresh advertisement is violative of
their right guaranteed to them under Articles 14 and 16 of
the Constitution of India but also in a way infringes upon
their right to seek parity in the matter of public
employment.
4. In this regard a specific grievance has also been
made that the State Government has been also indulging
into hostile discrimination among the same set of PRS in
the sense that in some of the District including
Aurangabad, Samastipur, Muzaffarpur, Sitamarhi the
striking PRS alike the petitioners in these cases have been
directed to be taken back in service, whereas the
petitioners have been shown the door.

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 86
5. Per contra, Mr. Ashok Kumar Chaudhary,
learned AAG 13, having filed an exhaustive counter
affidavit has sought to explain that by virtue of
assignment of duty of the post of PRS in DRDA it is an
out and out temporary working arrangement to supervise
the sponsored scheme of MNREGA of the Central
Government in which the State Government has to
achieve the object of providing minimum wages to the
people at the grassroot level of the villages. He explains
that on account of complete cessation of work by the PRS
all over the State for a period of more than four months
when the Government found that these Centrally
Sponsored Government Schemes were like to create a
serious problem for the common masses, the Government
after engaging itself and also agreeing to improve the
terms and conditions of working PRS, having found no
effective response from them in the matter of calling of
their strike had to take this step of terminating the services
of non-reporting PRS on duty in different district of all

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 87
over the State but also make immediate arrangement for
filling up those post again by issuance of an
advertisement.
6. In the considered opinion of this Court there can
be no two views that in a scheme like the one in which the
PRS are at present engaged for monitoring the success of
MNREGA Scheme there would be hardly a scope for
regularization of service which was the main demand of
PRS. By-now it is well settled that for regularization there
has to be not only the existence of vacant sanctioned and
permanent post but also the need of the employer to
maintain such permanent strength of the employees. The
Government is on record by way of its counter affidavit
that MNREGA is a Central Govenrment Sponsored
Scheme which, inter alia, provides that adult members of
a family has to be provided 100 days work and
remuneration to be paid to them is provided in the ratio of
90% from the Central Government and 10% from the
State Government. The MNREGA Scheme was in fact

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 88
launched for the benefit of needy people and also in
public interest. Thus, for managing this Scheme if the
Government had made specific representation to engage
employees on contractual basis including on the post of
PRS by entering into agreement which was initially for a
period of two years and had envisaged payment of fixed
amout of Rs.2000/- per month, the periodical extension
given to such scheme by the Central Government on year
to year basis will clothe no right to any of the contractual
employee including PRS to claim regularization of
service. As a matter of fact such regularization would not
only be contrary to the spirit of MNREGA Scheme but
also in excess of power of making such commitment on
the part of the State Government which has to depend
largely on the contribution of the Central Government.
7. This Court in fact also will go to hold that the
decision of the State Government to reject the demand of
the Association of PRS for regularization of their service
was legally permissible and correct because in the nature

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 89
of MNREGA Scheme, as it exists on date, there can be no
concept of permanency. The State Government is not
expected to employ people on permanent basis and create
a situation of financial deficit for satisfying the set of
contractual employees. The only difficulty, however, will
be that while in the case of PRS this benefit has been
denied, in some of the other similar contractual
appointment the Government has yielded to the demand
of regularization of service. It is here that the seed of
disparity and discontent has been sown by the State
Government itself.
8. Probably time has now come for the State
Government to identify the area in which it is under
constitutional duty to maintain a regular permanent
strength of the employees vis-à-vis its commitment to
other schemes requiring only engaging people on
contractual/ temporary basis. In the field of health and
education the concept of contractual employee may belie
the responsibility which is enjoined upon the Government,

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 90
whether Central or State, to introduce the concept of
contractual employment when either under Article 21 or
21A of the Constitution of India a fundamental right has
been given to each and every citizen to have either right to
life or right to have elementary basic education.
9. It is true that there are fields in which there
were/are still the possibility of continuance only in
temporary employment. If a bridge has to be constructed
the Government may need to employ the people what was
earlier known as an engagement on muster roll basis and
is now being sought to be translated into also MNREGA
Schemes. The State being the welfare State it has to take
many of such measures which can only be of temporary in
the sense that its work force and attention has to be
diverted from one sphere to other. When the Government
wants to find employment in the rural area for every
member of a family in the villages, that by itself has to be
as per the modality of such policy. Patently the work force
for monitoring such MNREGA scheme can never be a

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 91
permanent post because at the end of the day the
regulatory role of the State in monitoring these schemes
either directly or through its agencies internal or
outsourced will depend on the policy of the Government.
10. Based on these broader principles when this
Court would find that in 2007 what was offered to P.R.S.
was consolidated amount of Rs.2000/- per month which
has now been sought to be enhanced to Rs.6681/- per
month though it may not hold that the same is very
reasonable or matching the price index or market index
but then the Government has to also manage its own
resources not only on the front of providing employment
to P.R.S but also stand to its larger commitments to secure
fundamental right to its citizen as guaranteed under part
III of the Constitution of India.
11. As a matter of fact whatever explanations have
been given by the State Government in the counter
affidavit filed in C.W.J.C.No. 15219/2015 being the main
case would go to show that efforts were being made by

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 92
the Government to settle the issue with the Association of
PRS since January, 2015 and certain measures were also
taken wherein not only the monthly fixed remuneration
was fixed at Rs.6681/- but also they were made entitled to
protection of EPF Scheme as has been stated in
paragraphs no. 11 and 12 of the counter affidavit
supported by documentary evidence by way of
Government decision.
12. In this background if the Government had
taken step for terminating the services of PRS who
continued on strike for a period of more than four months
and thus, brought the whole MNREGA Scheme to a
grinding halt, the Government decision to terminate the
services of PRS cannot be held to be bad either on fact or
in law. Right to indulge into peaceful agitation does not
necessarily mean right to also go on strike inasmuch as
resorting to such a strike goes against the very root of
democracy. Strike was in fact the concept in the industrial
employment which was once recognized even by the

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 93
courts in cases arising out of Industrial Disputes Act. Such
right to go on strike will never flow in a completely
different situation like the present one to P.R.S., being not
at par what has been recognized under section 22 of the
Industrial Disputes Act which in Chapter V deals
exclusively with regard to provision of the strikes and
lock-outs in the field of employment in the industries.
13. Whether such a strike can be made applicable
in the present monitoring of MNREGA Schemes by P.R.S
is the key question in these writ petitions. According to
this Court PRS are not covered by the provision of
Industrial Disputes Act and if they so claim their remedy
will be somewhere else and not before the Writ Court.
Learned counsel for the petitioners in fact also have
conceded that as per terms and conditions of contractual
employment offered to PRS for monitoring MNREGA
Scheme they are not strictly covered by the provisions of
the Industrial Disputes Act.
14. It was in this background that this Court had

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 94
requested Mr. Pradeep Kumar, Secretary to the Rural
Development Department, to appear and assist this Court
to evolve a mechanism for finding some sort of solution
arising out of strike of P.R.S., stoppage of MNREGA
scheme and eventual termination of services of P.R.S. Mr.
Kumar has explained to this Court that on account of
illegal strike the State Government has largely suffered to
the extent of providing sufficient number of man-days for
employment in MNREGA Scheme in the present financial
year, inasmuch as during 2014-15 2,71,38,658 man-days
were created but on account of strike of PRS during April,
May, June and July it has came down to 1,42,36,553 man-days in the year, 2015-16 and that on account of this strike
of PRS even upto the month of September, 2015 only
1,61,74,008 man-days of employment could be created, as
a result of which financial target of the State Government
of Rs.77,508.82 could be spent against the target of
Rs.1,43,001.70. He has also explained that in no view of
the matter the provision of Contingency Fund can be

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 95
increased beyond 6% under MNREGA Scheme.
15. This Court having thus noticed the broader
outline of the MNREGA scheme and the chaotic
condition created on account of strike of P.R.S., all over
the State is also of the view that painful decision to
terminate the service of PRS was backed by the public
interest. Such a complete stoppage of work by PRS and
their going on strike, therefore, can be also easily held to
be not only contrary to the terms and conditions of the
agreement to which each and every PRS are signatory at
the time of getting contractual employment but is also
contrary to the spirit of abandoning the work despite there
being a mutual clause of getting rid of each other by
giving notice of seven days. The right to seek parity in
public employment definitely flows from the Articles 14
and 16 of the Constitution of India but then that also has
its inherent limitation, one of them being nature of
employment.
16. The petitioners faced with this situation can

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 96
have no justification in either resorting to or continuance
with the strike because their very existence was for
monitoring the MNREGA Scheme and if the scheme itself
was at the peril of its becoming a source of
embarrassment, the resultant Government decision for
termination of service of P.R.S., and their replacement by
fresh set of contractual employees cannot be faulted either
on fact or in law. This Court in exercise of its power of
judicial review cannot enter into the realm of policy
making decision of the State and its limited concern
would be to save and secure the paramount public interest
which has to essentially outweigh the personal grievance
of PRS in securing better terms and conditions in their
service. Judged in this background this Court will also not
find any real merit in the plea of discrimination, inasmuch
as the Government has come out to say that in some of the
district including in Aurangabad only such PRS have been
taken back in service who had unconditional called off
their strike and had reported on duty.

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 97
17. Having thus regard to the complete scenario
that this Court has been made aware of that though the
law is not in favour of the petitioners but then keeping in
view of the long continued service of the petitioners for a
period of seven to eight years, the equity would be in their
favour. For coming to this conclusion, this Court would
find that certain expectations were created in the mind of
the P.R.S., that if other similarly situated contractual
employees including Panchayat Teachers, they could get
the benefit of not only the regular pay scale but also of
being placed in regular service primarily on account of
their continued agitation probably that was the way that
even P.R.S., could be given regular pay scale with assured
continued service. This Court is also mindful of the fact
that there is no allegation against the petitioners and/or
other P.R.S., of resorting to any violent means and that
their going on strike was completely peaceful by taking all
these facts into consideration, this Court would give one
time indulgence to all the terminated P.R.S., of the State

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 98
including the petitioners by way of one time reprieve on
wholly humanitarian and sympathetic consideration. This
indulgence, however, would be conditional by making it
clear to all the P.R.S., including the petitioners that they
would strictly abide by the terms and conditions of their
contractual engagement and they shall not again resort to
going on strike.
18. This Court would, accordingly, dispose of all
these writ applications by giving the following directions:
 (i) All the P.R.S. including the petitioners, whose
services have been terminated on account of their
resorting to and continuing with the strike, shall be taken
back in service by 31st October, 2015 provided they
immediately report on duty to their respective controlling
authorities with a written individual apology and
undertaking that they shall not again go on strike in future
and would strictly abide by the terms and conditions as set
out in their contractual agreement with DRDA.
(ii) Any of the PRS including the petitioners, who

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 99
shall not report on duty on or before 31st October, 2015,
with their aforesaid written apology and undertaking, shall
not be reinstated in service and their vacancies shall be
filled up by undergoing the fresh process of recruitment.
(iii) Those of the PRS including the petitioners, who
shall be taken back in service in view of their submission
of joining report with their written apology and
undertaking to their respective controlling authorities,
shall not be entitled for payment of any salary for the
period they have not worked during the period of strike
and till the date of their acceptance of joining in terms of
this order.
(iv) The advertisement which has been issued for
filling up the post of PRS on account of removal of the
petitioners and others on account of their remaining on
strike shall not be given effect to till 31st of October, 2015
and only those required number of post of PRS on the
basis of such advertisement shall be filled up after 31st
October, 2015 which is found to be necessary for

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 100
monitoring of MNREGA scheme after reinstatement of
the petitioners and other PRS all over the State.
(v) Though any and every PRS including the
petitioners would not be deprived of raising their
legitimate grievance for a better service condition in
future but their such agitation will also be peaceful and
also without adversely affecting the MNREGA Schemes
in which they have been engaged.
(vi) The State Government, if need be in
consultation with the Central Govenrment, must make its
sincere effort to consider the genuine grievances raised by
individual PRS or through Association of PRS to ensure
periodical revision of their fixed monthly wages alike it
has been done in the recent past by enhancing their fixed
monthly emoluments and remuneration to the tune of
Rs.6,681/- per month.
(vii) The Secretary to the Rural Development
Department shall issue a general direction to all the
Collector of the District and the Deputy Development

Patna High Court CWJC No.15219 of 2015 (3) dt.06-10-2015 101
Commissioner monitoring the MNREGA Scheme being
carried through respective District Rural Development
Agency for the compliance of the directions given in this
order without exception and its observance in letter and
spirit.
19. With the aforesaid observations and directions,
these applications are disposed of.
 20. Let a copy of this order be given to Mr. Ashok
Kumar Chaudhary, learned AAG-13 for its being
communicated to all concern including Mr. Pradeep
Kumar, the Secretary to Rural Development Department,
for its compliance in letter and spirit.
21. The personal appearance of Mr. Pradeep Kumar,
Secretary, Rural Development Department is also
dispensed with.
Ranjan/-
 (Mihir Kumar Jha, J)
U
